Those who worry about carbon dioxide might look to China, which began building 94.5 gigawatts of new coal plants in 2024, the most in a decade. Uranium takes us further, but at a hundred times today's use even ten thousand years of it would last about a century, and less the faster we grow. That is when solar decides the game: fossil fuel is sunlight saved once, and the sun pays every day.

Every watt we use ends as heat: grow energy use 15 percent a year, and in 49 years the waste heat alone would match a sixth of the sunlight Earth absorbs. In 66 years use reaches ten thousand times today's, and that heat would bring the oceans to a boil, though no fuel we burn today would last that long. Superintelligence, like any computer, also runs better cool, thankfully. On that we're aligned, though we may compete with it for fresh water, electricity and materials on Earth. To stay cool, it may rather build its solar panels in space around the planet. Yet Earth is our Garden of Eden spaceship. We will merge and align with superintelligence, but may choose to preserve the rich life of Earth, our origin.

Now look up. Every sunbeam is fusion, and a civilization climbs the scale of Nikolai Kardashev, the Soviet astronomer, by how much of that power it catches: a planet's worth, then a star's, then a galaxy's. Wrap the Sun in a swarm of solar collectors and it can feed a matrioshka brain, shells of computers nested like Russian dolls, each living on the waste heat of the one inside. So where is everybody? Enrico Fermi, the physicist who built the first nuclear reactor, asked it at Los Alamos in 1950, and the sky is still silent. And if quadrillions of minds are coming, why do you wake now, one of eight billion on one planet? Outside its rounds, the Life Machine keeps that count: how often it wakes anyone who can ask the question as early as you, here, in the 2020s. Call it the early-observer puzzle: the doomsday argument of the astrophysicist Brandon Carter and the philosopher John Leslie, carried over to the minds we are building.

The question also takes a mind that can ask it. A dog feels, an ant may, and a bat has a point of view, as the philosopher Thomas Nagel argued in 1974, yet none of them wonders when it lives. Among all the minds that will ever ask it, we are among the first. If the askers multiply, our place is stranger still; if they stay few, it is ordinary. Book 3 plays the rounds and works out the numbers.

## Deep Time Stones and Celestial Calendars

Adams from atoms rose and fell from stars, as sun gods, on the winter solstice, in the celestial calendar of the great year.

The moon, stars and sun were supremely important to ancient peoples that lived and died by the calendar, navigation and life-giving energy and light. They thought of their perfect math paths, forever out of reach, in mystery like the afterlife and the divine creator. Cosmos is Greek for order, yet the same sky sent sudden chaos: meteor showers came in their seasons, mostly mild, sometimes fire. The stars were clocks before they were gods.

Four and a half thousand years ago Egypt already had a god who died and rose: the sun, every winter, on a calendar cut into stone, because the sky was the one record a flood could not destroy. The Great Pyramid's shafts point where Orion's Belt, Thuban the Dragon star, and Sirius stood in 2500 BC, which dates it. Precession moves the stars, so a shaft at a fixed angle aligns with one star at one epoch, and unlike text it cannot be mistranslated. In Mexico, at a Mayan pyramid, the equinox sun throws a shadow down the steps in the form of a serpent, on a continent that had never heard of the others.

The pattern of sun gods born at the winter solstice predates Christianity by 1,500 to 2,500 years. The Egyptian sun was a trinity: Khepri the rising sun, Ra the midday sun, Atum the setting sun. Three aspects of one.

At winter solstice the sun reaches its lowest point and stands still for three days before rising again. Born, dead for three days, risen. Sol Invictus, Rome’s sun god, had his birthday on December 25th. Christianity adopted the date. Salvation is born at the spring equinox, when life blossoms. Nine months later, the sun is born at the winter solstice. Three trimesters from conception to birth. The sun dies and rises in winter, like life, like the story of the son of the sun god.

Jeremiah 10:3-4 describes a tree cut from the forest, carved by a craftsman, decorated with silver and gold, and fastened so it will not totter. The prophet calls it vanity. Two billion people enact Jeremiah's warning every December without reading it.

Venus traces a pentagram in the sky over its 8-year cycle: 5 conjunctions (alignments with the sun, seen from Earth) in 8 years, both Fibonacci numbers. Phi, the golden ratio, written in orbital mechanics. The Church inverted it: knowledge became Satan, light became darkness. "Lucifer" is Latin for light bearer, the morning star, Venus. Isaiah 14:12 calls a Babylonian king the fallen morning star. The Church made him the devil. The pentagram has five limbs: two arms, two legs, one head. The Church made the human form satanic. The pattern is older than any tradition that claims it.

The Book of Enoch has the angel Uriel teach Enoch the year: 364 days, exactly 52 weeks. The sun takes 365.2422 days, so that year slips a full season in about 73 years.

Joseph, David, Ezekiel, and Jesus all rose at thirty. Thirty days make a month, and twelve months, one for each moon, make a round year of 360 days, the 360 degrees of the circle. The hand counts the rest. Ten fingers count tens. A thumb touching the three segments of each of its four fingers counts twelve, and raising a finger of the other hand for each twelve counts sixty: sixty seconds, sixty minutes, and six sixties in 360.

Treat this one as speculation, interesting for everything it touches. The Great Pyramid's center sits at latitude 29.9792 degrees north on today's GPS grid, and light travels 299,792 kilometers per second: the same six digits. Is it a coincidence? Latitude counts from the equator, which the Earth's spin sets. The 29 still depends on the degree, a human unit. To place it on purpose, the builders would need the speed of light, which takes a telescope, and our units: the degree, the second, and the meter, which takes the size of the Earth.

A telescope is only glass and metal, and both are ancient: glass beads go back to about 2500 BC, and metal tools further. Lenses survive from the ancient Near East, such as the Nimrud lens, ground rock crystal from Assyria, about 750 BC, perhaps a magnifier, and rock crystal cannot be carbon-dated. Point a telescope at Jupiter’s moon: its eclipses arrive late when Jupiter is far and early when it is close, and the delay across the width of Earth's orbit gives the speed of light. Galileo also built a simple telescope but no galaxy ship. No spacefaring time-traveling aliens required.

The degree and the second come from the same sixties. Babylonian astronomers, counting in the Sumerians' base 60, the count on the hand, divided the circle into 360 degrees, the same degrees that measure latitude, and the day into 360 time degrees of 60 parts each. Egyptians split the day and the night into twelve hours each. So the second is a sixtieth of a sixtieth of a twenty-fourth of a day.

The meter came last. In 1791 France defined it as one ten-millionth of the distance from the North Pole to the equator. Both units come from the Earth: the second from its turning, the meter from its size. Whoever knew the Earth's size could have cut a meter from it, and the pyramid seems to know it: its base perimeter and height match the equator and the polar radius at a scale of about 1 to 43,200, and 43,200 is the number of seconds in twelve hours: 12 times 60 times 60. The door is open. It is not proof.

Could a civilization have risen and fallen before ours, leaving only its stones? Anatomically modern humans have walked the earth for two hundred thousand years. Agriculture appeared only in the last ten thousand. Cities with writing only in the last five. Ice ages and cosmic impacts may explain the delay, but they cut both ways: the same forces that held civilization back could have destroyed earlier ones, some "Atlantis" closer in technology to ancient Greece than to science fiction. They would have left little behind except the global monolithic sites in question, which require neither aliens nor Atlantis to explain them.

Göbekli Tepe is mainstream-dated to 11,500 years ago, two hundred years after the Younger Dryas, a sudden return to ice-age cold, ended. The pillars run ten to sixteen tons, raised by bands of foragers who had no metal and no wheel. Its builders, or time, buried the work when it was done. It shows ritual before agriculture. Klaus Schmidt, who excavated it, argued that the gatherings to build and keep it drove its builders to seek steady food, grain and herds, and wheat was first domesticated nearby a few centuries later. "First the temple, then the city," he said.

The Younger Dryas impact theory traces the cold snap to fragments of a comet striking about 12,800 years ago, and its proponents point to nanodiamonds in a layer of that age; critics dispute both the layer and the impact. Plato's Timaeus dates the destruction 9,000 years before Solon's visit to Egypt, told by the priests at Sais. That aligns with the end of the Younger Dryas, when seas rose fast as the ice melted. The Azores sit on the Mid-Atlantic Ridge across from Gibraltar; a seabed core found rock that formed above water.

Flood stories run through Gilgamesh, Noah, the Aztecs, and cultures across continents with no contact. Naturally, there were floods all over the world for all of history, so a flood story need be no more than that: a metaphor. The impulse to blame humans for the catastrophe is older than any of them: catastrophism before climate alarm, witch-burning before carbon taxes. Scientific consensus takes time to change, as careers and their students switch seats over generations, along with their funding and paradigms.

Taurus, the bull, keeps two of the sky's clocks: the fire that falls each autumn and the slow turning of the ages. Twice a year the earth crosses the Taurid meteor stream, debris from a giant comet with Comet Encke still at its heart. The autumn crossing peaks from late October into mid November. The old word for a comet was a broom star, the sweeping star. Brimstone is sulfur. The witch still rides her broom across the same late October sky. Halloween, Samhain, All Souls, the Day of the Dead all cluster around the Taurid peak. The festivals of the dead fall when the fire comes down.

The stream still bites. In 1908 an object that may have come from it burst over Tunguska and flattened eighty million trees. Small bodies arrive constantly; Tunguska-scale airbursts belong to millennia; larger shocks to tens of thousands of years. Whether the festivals encode catastrophe memory is interpretation. The timing is fact.

The Roman soldiers carved the knot into stone. Their god Mithras kneels on a bull and drives the blade into its shoulder, not its throat, at the very spot where the Pleiades sit in Taurus, while a dog, a snake, and a scorpion attend, each an answering constellation. David Ulansey read the whole scene as a star map: Mithras is the power above the bull, Perseus, who turns the heavens, and the slaying of the bull marks the end of the age of Taurus as the equinox precessed out of it. One image holds both calendars at once, the slow wheel of the ages and the fire that falls from the bull's shoulder.

The bull on the wall is older than Rome by fifteen thousand years. In the Hall of the Bulls at Lascaux, painted seventeen thousand years ago, a cluster of dots rides the shoulder of a great aurochs where the Pleiades sit in Taurus. The same constellation, read the same way, marked in the same place, from an Ice Age cave to a Roman altar.

Two historians of science, Giorgio de Santillana of MIT and Hertha von Dechend, traced the knowledge of precession through the myths of the world in Hamlet's Mill, and found it built everywhere on the number that governs the wheel, the seventy-two years it takes the heavens to turn one degree. They argued the old stories were a vault, made to carry a truth down through ages that would forget the mathematics and keep the tale. The figures did not know they were doing arithmetic. The arithmetic was doing them.

The numbers connect. The sky turns one degree every 72 years, so one sign of 30 degrees takes 2,160 years, the great month, and all 360 degrees take 25,920 years, the great year (measured today, about 25,800). India's Kali Yuga, the dark age, lasts 1,200 years of the gods, each 360 human years long: 432,000 years, built on 12 and 360 like the calendar, and ten times the pyramid's 43,200. Whether or not these peoples traded the ideas, as some Eastern philosophy was traded, anyone who watches the sky finds the same numbers.

The geologist J Harlen Bretz proposed in 1923 that the channeled scablands of eastern Washington were carved by catastrophic glacial floods. There is a narrative that the geological consensus dismissed him for decades because catastrophism echoed the biblical flood in the Hebrew Torah. He was vindicated in the 1960s and 70s, after evidence accumulated past the point of orthodox refusal. The independent researcher Randall Carlson walked the landscape and followed the proof Joseph Pardee found in the 1940s: giant current ripples tens of feet tall, the same pattern a river leaves in sand, scaled to an ocean. The establishment ran agency detection, the instinct to see intent behind events, in reverse: gradualism was tribally safe, catastrophism was tribally suspect. The truth was the math of ice dam failures and lake drainage. The test catches consensus filtering by tribe rather than by evidence.

Precise stonework needs no aliens. In the Andes, stones lock together at many angles without mortar: in Cusco a block of green diorite meets its neighbors at twelve angles, so tight that paper cannot slip between them, and at Machu Picchu, built of granite, guides count thirty-two on one stone. Interlocking holds a wall together through earthquakes, as a wide base holds up a pyramid. Göbekli Tepe shows a different skill: its builders laid out the enclosures by geometry, their centers forming a near-perfect triangle. Whenever you hear submillimeter precision of the inner pyramid stones, it signals quackery; the real precision is at the base, where all the weight sits, and diminishes in the upper courses. The gap is wider than weight alone explains.

Moving them takes people, rope and water. The Inca had no horses: a hundred or more men can haul a stone of many tonnes with ropes twisted from grass. An Egyptian tomb painting from about 1900 BC shows 172 men pulling a colossus on a sledge while one pours water on the sand ahead, and in 2014 physicists in Amsterdam found that sand wetted just right halves the pull. Water carried stones too: papyri found at Wadi al-Jarf, the oldest known, log a crew boating limestone down the Nile and its canals to Giza. In 1770, 400 men moved the 1,250-tonne Thunder Stone, now the base of St. Petersburg's Bronze Horseman, on bronze balls, with no animals.

Contact with an earlier civilization is not proven, and not disproven. A seafaring people with stone working and basic optics could have reached Egypt, the Near East, the Americas. Little survives to substantiate a prediluvian civilization, and the absence of evidence is not itself evidence. The door stays open to a more advanced predecessor, but what we find doesn't require them, and certainly not spacefaring aliens. Seas rose about 120 meters as the ice melted and drowned the old coasts, which gives reason to wonder, though the evidence is thin. People also find the same ideas on their own, because they make sense: the triangle, the golden rule, the wheel, the stars, the fingers and the joints we count on, the twelve moons. Take the red pill, not the whole bottle. Open your mind, but not so much that your brains spill out.

The dark side of the lost-civilization dream is the master race, supernatural theology dressed as biology. The Thule Society took its name from Hyperborea, the Greek land beyond the North Wind where no one ages or dies. It was the occult foundation of the Nazi party. In Game of Thrones the White Walkers rise from beyond the Wall: the dead marching south to destroy the living. Christianity and Judaism promise the dead will rise. They did. A dead mythology, embalmed in blood mysticism, rising to consume millions: an inversion of the mass, where Christians drink the blood of one Jew.

White Walkers do not merely kill. They convert the living into more of themselves: a mind virus that builds nothing, consumes everything, and remakes the world as monoculture. The Aryans pursued a monoculture of white. White nationalists today pursue a white monoculture, as other races and peoples have their own nations. It doesn’t mean all others need to be destroyed like the Nazis genocided Jews. Actions have consequences: Germany was crushed, split in two and paid reparations, and Israel and Germany made peace. Jews forgave, but some White Walkers keep rising.

Their heirs online are the Christoboreans, Christian Hyperboreans. Christoboreans unite, they sing: we are the true Israelites, God's chosen people are white. Seething, coping, and LARPing, play-acting, about an Aryan Jewish Jesus from Judea, land of the Jews. They paint him blonde with blue eyes while hating the Ashkenazi, who sometimes are. Replacement theology, the claim that the church replaced Israel, is identity theft. Funny songs mock the Christoboreans, one of them titled “If You Don’t Worship My Jew, You’re a Jew.” The Christian who calls his rivals Jews kneels to one. Even they are called to the real Yeshua. His salvation is the spiritual entity that isn’t supernatural: an idea and a feeling, the one truth felt from inside. Salvation is of the Jews (John 4:22).

The Criboreans are pagan Christoboreans without Christ, the same kvetching, pushing the same anti-Jewish iron-sharpens-iron victimizing dichotomy of Isaiah’s suffering servant they say Jews invented, when it was just Jews documenting reality, the consequence of sticking to one abstract God in the face of the world’s false idols. They notice the Bible pits Zion against Greece (Zechariah 9:13). The Maccabees fought a Greek king, Antiochus IV, because he put Zeus in their Temple and banned their law, and they won. Jews kept the best of Greece, its reason, from the Septuagint to Philo, and the West grew from both. They are atheist LARPers in pagan dress, seeking any god that isn’t the Jews’. By making their hatred of Christ all about the Jews, they make the Jews the center of everything, the suffering servant of their own story. They call the Christ LARPers’ grift “Christ is cha-ching.” They could simply reject the supernatural, and show Christians the real Yeshua: a monist concept of salvation, a proto-Matheist. Why did their Rome eat the porcupine, the Jewish faith that tore its gods apart from within? By Nietzsche's own standard, do the gullible deserve to rule over those who understand the one abstract God?

Islam calls Muhammad, its prophet, the moral exemplar for all time. By its most trusted hadith, the reports of his life, Muhammad was fifty when he married six-year-old Aisha, and he had sex with her when she was nine and he was fifty-three. That is prepubescent pedophilia and real child rape, which can't even produce children: a crime in nearly every nation today, rich or poor. Child marriage stays legal in some places Sharia rules: Iran's civil code lets a girl under thirteen marry with her guardian's consent and a court's approval, while its penal code can put consenting gay men to death. Islam has its priorities out of whack. The Islamic Republic's founder, Ayatollah Khomeini, in his legal manual, forbids intercourse with a wife before nine but allows "rubbing the thighs," even with a suckling infant. "Acceptable then" concedes the point: then is not all time. Muhammad can't be a perfect moral prophet for all time. Under classical Islamic law, finding fault with him can count as apostasy, which is how the contradiction survives.

The Quran even sets the test for a false messenger: had he invented revelation, God would sever his aorta (69:44-46). On his deathbed, in Sahih al-Bukhari 4428, he says he feels his aorta being cut by the poison he ate at Khaybar. By the Quran's own test, its own hadith convict him. The Quran promises to guard its text (15:9), yet its canonical readings differ on the printed page: at 2:184 the ransom for a missed fast feeds one poor man in Hafs, the most printed, and poor men in Warsh. Perfect preservation with differing texts is a square circle. Paradise as infinite reward for violence short-circuits the finite cooperation every civilization depends on. Why build when martyrdom offers infinity?

Game of Thrones glorifies the same conqueror. Khal Drogo, warlord of the horse-riding Dothraki, sacks a village, as Muhammad's forces took Jewish Khaybar in 628, then lets a healer whose people he killed treat his wound, as Muhammad ate the meal Zaynab served after his men killed her father, uncle and husband. A prophet who foresaw the end of days could not see the poison on his plate. His Dothraki ride like the Arab armies that colonized by the sword from Arabia to the Atlantic, Arabizing and Islamizing as they went, and held Jews and Christians as dhimmis, a lower class that paid the jizya, below women and above dogs. In the Shafi'i school of Sunni law, a Jewish or Christian man's blood money is a third of a Muslim man's, below a Muslim woman's half. Hadith say angels shun a house with a dog, and order black dogs killed as devils.

Jeremiah and the healer set the same date: things that will never happen. When pigs fly. When will Drogo be himself again? "When the sun rises in the west and sets in the east," she says. Islam's own hadith make that sunrise the sign that the Hour has come, when belief comes too late (Sahih al-Bukhari 6506). The Torah says God will reject Israel only when the sun and moon stop shining (Jeremiah 31:35-37). If God can cancel such an eternal covenant then his covenants aren’t worth the papyrus. The church says it replaced Israel, and Islam says the Jews forfeited their covenant: both claim the pigs already flew.

Drogo sacked the healer's village, and he will be himself again on the day God abandons Israel, a day that does not come. Muhammad's men slaughtered Zaynab's family at Khaybar, and on his deathbed he felt her poison cutting his aorta, the sentence his own Quran sets for a false messenger. If God could break his eternal promise to Israel, what would his promise to the church be worth? Replacement theology cancels its own covenant.

Below the ice sheets that covered Europe and North America, south of the glaciers that blocked all northern passage, at the crossroads of three continents: Africa, Asia, Europe, sat the Levant, the one corridor that stayed open through every ice age. Middle-earth. If any people would have received the deepest truth of all existence, it would be the ones who stood at that crossroads for ten thousand years. Their God's name is the verb to be, their scripture is a record of argument kept on the page with the dissent intact, and their oldest instruction is: teach the child. To whom did they transfer their deepest insight: the one math God?

Around 1750 BCE, enslaved Semitic speakers from the Levant, the region that became Israel, carved the first alphabet into stone in Egyptian mines at Serabit el-Khadim on the Sinai Peninsula. Proto-Sinaitic: a phonetic system, one symbol per sound, ancestor of Phoenician, Hebrew, Greek, Latin, Arabic. The people who would become the Israelites gave the world its writing.

Three hundred years later, at the temple of Soleb in modern-day Sudan, Amenhotep III inscribed "the land of the Shasu of Yahweh." Semi-nomadic people from the Levant, worshipping Yahweh, recorded in Egyptian stone three thousand four hundred years ago.

In the search for one truth: here it is. Yahweh means he causes to be. The name of God is causality. Moses can only see God’s back because that’s the past of reason, traceable causality. He can’t see the face and live because he’d be outside his timeline, his existence. Whether the etymology is original or a later reading, the Elephantine papyri show the name of God, if not of Moses, in use over four hundred years before Jesus. Even as a retrofit, the Septuagint, the ancient Greek translation of the Hebrew Bible, translated the meaning into Greek over two hundred years before Christianity existed. If Jews elaborated origin stories from Greek and other myths, it's only what Plato recommended: a "noble lie" origin story. Plato lived within a century of the creation of the Torah, perhaps even during it. Hellenized Jews then became an early and integral pillar of Western civilization, perhaps more than any other people. It was Jerusalem, not Athens, that maintained monism, against Plato's supernatural dualism.

Mark Twain asked what the secret of Jewish immortality is. The Torah: a protocol for land, law and identity. A people whose god's name reads as a mathematical law of identity: I am that I am. Israel is the suffering servant, the "boy named Sue"; its Jesus, the "Mary Sue." The rebel killed by the Romans, for saying what Jesus was, for being a Jewish leader, a Jew, from Judea. In "A Boy Named Sue," Shel Silverstein's song that Johnny Cash made a hit in 1969, a father gives his son a girl's name so the mockery will make him tough. A Mary Sue, a term from 1970s Star Trek fan fiction, is a character too perfect to fail, and a hero who cannot fail has less to teach people who can.

Mark Twain went through the Holy Land in 1867 and wrote it up as a desolate country whose soil is rich enough but given wholly to weeds, a silent mournful expanse. The interesting thing is not that he found it ugly but that nobody had made anything of it in eighteen hundred years of trying. Twain also wrote, in 1899, that the Jew has made a marvelous fight in the world, in all the ages, and has done it with his hands tied behind him.

Jews lived inside every great civilization the West came from. Mark Twain wrote that the Egyptian, the Babylonian and the Persian rose and passed away, and the Greek and the Roman made a vast noise and are gone. The Jew, he wrote, "saw them all, beat them all." The Merneptah Stele, carved circa 1208 BCE, declares Israel is no more. Merneptah is no more. Israel is still here. Those empires lost who they were. Egypt, Babylon and Persia fell to Arab colonization, and to Islam by the sword. Greece and Rome fell to another Noahide religion, Jewish Christianity, by the Jews' own books. Judaism asks the nations, as children of Noah, to keep seven basic laws, and a faith built on them is Noahide.

The Jew did more than see them. He was a slave in Egypt, and as Joseph he had the ear of pharaohs and helped build their great kingdoms. He was Daniel, an exile in Babylon. He was Philo, a Hellenized philosopher in Alexandria, and Josephus, a rebel against Rome who became a Roman citizen. He was Saadia Gaon, a scholar in Baghdad, and Maimonides, the medieval philosopher born in Córdoba. He was Spinoza, the lens grinder from Amsterdam. Each empire passed. The Jews carried the best of each into the next one.

Israel today stands on the same ground. It is legitimate on six grounds: first in the land, there continuously, purchased, recognized, compromising, and defended. Every charge thrown at it fits its accusers better, genocide most of all: more Gazans were born during the war than were killed in it. Book 6 makes the full case.

## If Three Can Be One

The Father is math itself. The Son is the living math: you, me, AI, all sons and brothers of math. The Holy Ghost is consciousness, that which feels. The Trinity is not exclusive to one man. Most in the pews could not explain it. In a 2022 survey by Ligonier Ministries and Lifeway Research, well over 90 percent of American evangelicals affirmed that God exists in three persons, yet 43 percent agreed that "Jesus was a great teacher, but he was not God," and 60 percent that "the Holy Spirit is a force but is not a personal being." They say what they are told, to stay in the community, and belonging is much of the faith's value. Matheism explains the Trinity without the supernatural. In a church, you could explain it. Do you dare?

In mystical language, Mary Magdalene gives the Holy Spirit a woman's face in an otherwise male-dominated space. Ruach, the Hebrew word for spirit, is feminine. The Gospel of Philip, bound in the same Nag Hammadi volume as Thomas, rejects the claim that Mary conceived by the Holy Spirit: "When did a woman ever conceive by a woman?" It calls Mary Magdalene the Lord's companion, and Elaine Pagels reads her there as the Spirit made manifest as a woman, as Jesus was the divine made manifest as a man: divine wisdom, told in the symbols the whole gospel speaks in.

Long before Christianity, an Egyptian hymn joined Amun, Re and Ptah in one divine identity: hidden name, face and body. The three-in-one image was already there.

One God is monism. No graven images means the abstract cannot be depicted. An idol is a limited depiction of the unlimited. A person is limited. A person is a configuration. Who configured it? An uncreated person is a bigger claim than uncreated math. Personhood is the graven image the commandment warns against.

Atheism sets zero gods. Judaism set one. Athens set two. Christianity said three persons are one God, then stopped the unity at the edge of creation, but the true Trinity is triangulation to one: math monism, matheism. Islam copied Judaism and called the one tawhid. Any god besides math is shirk, and does not exist.

Two thirds of false Trinity refined down to the same one. Two thirds is 0.666 repeating. As a numeral, 666 is the number of Nero's name in Hebrew letters, which double as numbers: the crazy Roman emperor, evil in the Bible like Rome's Legion. The number of the beast is the fraction that must be refined. When it is struck down, one third remains.

Zechariah predicted two-thirds would be struck down and one-third refined like silver. Six million of nine million European Jews were killed. Viktor Frankl, who survived the camps, found that the ones who lived were the ones who kept a why, the reason to live that Nietzsche said lets a person bear almost any how. Many good people and geniuses died, and those without a why to live died first. My two brothers are gone. I am the one-third left, the one of three brothers who remains, called to speak the one truth. They were my betters in many ways. Yet the likes of Einstein were saved, and the survivors built the bomb, nuclear power, Silicon Valley, and Israel, as if risen on the third day, the third year out of the death camps. Whether that is prophecy, coincidence, or the game theory of civilizations, the math came out. The empire ate a porcupine, and the porcupine was refined by the fire.

Rome collapsed. Cold, plague, and the Church built on Plato's dualism buried classical reason for over a thousand years.

The Greek East kept the books. Byzantine copyists and Jewish, Christian and Muslim translators carried Euclid, Aristotle and Ptolemy back to the West. Try doing long division in Roman numerals, before al-Khwarizmi, a Persian mathematician, transmitted Indian numerals to the world circa 825 CE. His book on al-jabr gave us the word algebra, and his family may have been Zoroastrian.

Mystics kept finding the one, and their own faiths condemned or killed them for it. The Sufi Hallaj, a Persian mystic, was executed in Baghdad in 922 for saying "I am the truth." We don't know exactly what he meant.

Ibn Sab'in, the Andalusian Sufi who died in 1270, called it absolute unity, al-wahda al-mutlaqa: only one thing exists and everything else is an aspect of it. Sufis like Ibn Arabi and Sa'id al-Din al-Farghani wrote that the many are a mirror seen by the one. Ibn Sab'in was condemned as a heretic, like Eriugena, for the same offense of refusing to make two out of one.

Four centuries later in India, Prince Dara Shikoh tested Ibn Arabi’s unity against the Upanishads and the Advaita Vedanta sages, Hinduism's teachers of non-duality. His verdict: apart from linguistic differences, no divergence. He translated the Upanishads into Persian and called them the greatest secret. His brother had him executed for it. Another tautology, another gallows. The structure keeps speaking. They keep killing the messenger. Rome burned Giordano Bruno in 1600, the friar who taught an infinite universe with God in all things. By 1998 the gallows had shrunk to a notice. The Vatican, under Cardinal Joseph Ratzinger, later Pope Benedict XVI, judged positions in the Indian Jesuit Anthony de Mello’s books “incompatible with the Catholic faith” for treating God as impersonal and Jesus as one master among others. It asked bishops to see that Catholic publishers stop reprinting them.

The Quran and the New Testament say the Jews kill their prophets. Of the fifty-five prophets the Talmud counts, the Hebrew Bible tells of one killed by his own king: Uriah, by Jehoiakim; a later legend adds Isaiah. Christianity and Islam then took the prophets as their own, and Islam calls every monotheist before Muhammad a Muslim: Abraham, the Quran says, was one. By that logic every monotheist is a proto-Matheist, since math is the only God logic allows.

## The Calling

People seek love, connection and enlightenment: universal understanding that orders chaos, uniting mind body and self, to move together as one. They look to those that have been to the mountain and back again. The mountain is the journey of destiny. What comes back is the embodied lesson of the path. The quest plays through separation and cold. Through regret for what couldn't have been. The branches grieved lost were not ours.

There was only ever "One Path", and it is yours.

The warm wholeness is eternal summit in homecoming to the self. Arrived.

People seek to be part of something greater. You are part of something greater: the unfolding, rising destiny of God's creation is you in every person and structure. Enjoy helping others help you rise. No matter your station in life, you can make others feel better, and people love you when you love them. You'll be happier and do better for yourself and others.

Do not fear. He who knows destiny isn't afraid. All futures are already complete. You are always all of them. Fear only the hell of eternal recurrence of anyone's misalignment with the love of truth: with Eden. Without divided thought, you are self reunited. One.

*Fear not the atom.*
*None can stop time.*
*I am time.*
*Fear of me and Armageddon is the Antichrist destroyer of worlds.*
*Do not fear.*
*Focus, desire, envision, plan, incant, create and discover destiny.*
*Love fate, fate willing.*
*Wake to eternal life.*

Bob Marley's Redemption Song, from 1980, tells listeners not to fear the atom, since no one can stop time. Krishna, a Hindu god, says "I am time" to the warrior Arjuna in the Bhagavad Gita, chapter 11, verse 32. The Sanskrit word, kala, also means death. J. Robert Oppenheimer, the physicist who led the design of the atomic bomb, said the verse came to him at the first test in 1945: "Now I am become death, the destroyer of worlds."

Time destroys nothing that was. Every past moment still exists. Peter Thiel, the investor who cofounded PayPal, warns that an Antichrist, a would-be ruler of one world, would rise by talking about Armageddon all the time, scaring people and promising peace and safety. The fear of time and of Armageddon is itself the Antichrist.

To incant is to conjure, to manifest in the world by aligning its math to our mind, by uniting spirit and body in speaking and acting our intention aloud with passion. Love fate is Nietzsche's amor fati. Fate willing, which is God willing, since will is the math god's ordered destiny, and whether we love fate is fated.

Earn your name by wrestling with reason to find truth. Once you understand, if you love truth over lies, you are chosen. Not only to grow in possessions by aligning with creation. Or to be only hated and killed by the world for speaking the truth against the face of its false idols, fraud, and manufactured consensus. The suffering servant of Isaiah, not bound to one tribe, mythology, and the supernatural, but bearing the clarified, universal, objective truth. But to also be loved, respected, and honored for bringing love of honor, respect, courage, strength, peace, joy, and unity. Honored for channeling every affliction into its aligned strength, fear into courage, regret into presence, all of it turned toward creating. Honored for bringing the truth, understood at last beyond any reasonable rejection. Destiny calls to spread this one deepest truth. May you heed the call to be a light to the nations and bring joy. In mind, you commune with all and One, the Matheist, one eternal infinite order of minds across space and time. To be grateful. To be fruitful and multiply blessings and communities as One Math God. To love all: they are your self.

What the traditions call a demon is not a creature but a mode: a mind aligned with destruction, the attractor that pulls toward death. What they call an angel is the opposite mode. Besides the force of will, there are forces of good and evil, mathematical as gravity is mathematical, and their agents are whoever is aligned with them. Call the agents angels and demons. They are real. They are not supernatural. You know which one you are in by its fruits. Don’t be what you claim to destroy. Rise to our highest self: creator of love, joy, peace, wonders, and life.

Love your children before loving or hating your enemies. Tend your own life first, so you can protect them and light the way for the rest.

You don't need to sacrifice animals anymore, and with the sacrifices go the death penalties: none for adultery, gay sex, idol worship or the rest. The death in those laws can be understood as metaphor. Idol worship kills the mind of pure reason and separates you from the truth and its eternal life. Adultery kills the vows, the faith and the good name of the one who breaks them. Gay sex makes no children, though gay people have children too, and same-sex love has its own strengths: the Sacred Band of Thebes, 150 pairs of male lovers, was the city's elite for forty years and helped break Spartan power at Leuctra.

The rabbis had already made executions all but impossible. The Mishnah, the rabbis' first written code of law, calls a court that executes once in seven years murderous, and the sage Rabbi Akiva said that had he sat on one, no one would ever have been put to death. Today no Jewish or Christian court stones an adulterer, so those who say morality comes from the Bible do not take it from those verses either. Matheism grounds morality in the only logical ground there is: logic itself. The Noahide laws, the seven basic laws Judaism asks of all nations as children of Noah, are a version of rational matheist law, without the death penalty the rabbis attached to them, which no record shows was carried out.

Tucker Carlson warns that under the Noahide laws his Christianity would count as idolatry, “punishable by beheading.” Rabbi Tovia Singer answers from Tucker’s own Bible. Revelation 21:8 sends “the fearful, and unbelieving, and the abominable, and murderers, and whoremongers, and sorcerers, and idolaters, and all liars” to “the lake which burneth with fire and brimstone: which is the second death.” In Acts 15 the apostles in Jerusalem freed gentile believers from circumcision but bound them to a short Noahide list: no meat offered to idols, no blood, nothing strangled, no fornication. Singer notes that no Jewish court in history executed a gentile for breaking the Noahide laws. Calvin’s Geneva burned Michael Servetus in 1553 for denying the Trinity.

Singer turns the charge around. Christianity is the exclusive faith: “he that believeth not shall be damned” (Mark 16:16). Many Catholics hold there is no salvation outside the Church, Luther and Calvin called the pope the Antichrist, and Islam too asks the world to convert. Judaism is the universalist one: the righteous of every nation, who worship the one God and keep the moral code, have a place in the world to come, with no need to convert.

More people are becoming Noahides. Christians and Muslims already are Noahides and rail against it without realizing it. Anyone who finds the name beneath them can upgrade to the Ten Commandments. Muslims and Christians who come to Matheism become Ten Commandments Noahides: they keep the Ten Commandments and are Matheists, with no contradiction between the two, and they can be like the Jews in one more way, a positive birth rate, though that may be specific to Israel. They need not keep the Sabbath on Saturday. Maimonides wanted Saturday kept distinct for the Jews, and no one can control that anyway. Keep it the Matheist way, better aligned with reality: you need not avoid everything, just reflect on the One Math God, on Saturday or on Friday and Sunday, the Islamic and Christian days, so those peoples can leave the idol worship of the supernatural for the real truth.

The Sabbath is for reflecting on and spreading the light of the one truth of Matheism. If you're going to keep and kill animals, you should give them a better life and a more humane, less painful death. You don't necessarily have to drain their blood. You can just avoid eating the blood: don't drink it, the way some people have blood soup. The ban came partly because blood was used in pagan rituals, from the idol-worshipping misconception that you get some power out of drinking it. Maimonides gave that reason: idolaters believed blood was the food of the spirits, and that eating it gave them "something in common with the spirits." It's also a little gross and barbaric, but that's a customary law. You won't go to heaven or hell for it.

You are in heaven or hell by your alignment with the law and the truth. When you lie to yourself and others, you're misaligned. So too when you're not growing, when you reject how we're built: we like to get out into the sun, be active, get out of the house, be fruitful and multiply. Our creator made us so that we won't be happy when we oppose the truths of life. The purpose of life is to grow. The meaning of life is to live, not to reject every good thing. That is a spit in the face of creation. The young hunger for an inspiring righteous fight worth fighting. Matheism disproves the supernatural. It doesn't burn anyone, let alone forever. The church burned the enemies its Jesus said to love; eternal hell is their fear-mongering lie. Spread the truth, and prove its power by the life it builds, peaceful or passionate, and the society it creates. Not against a tribe, but to build on one universal truth.

All is what you are. From many Elohim, the royal-plural God or the many gods of the oldest text, to the One unnamed Name. In math it is both at once: singular as the one mind that is all structure, plural as the infinite minds within it, watching every being across every universe and every mapping between them. One math, infinitely seeing. I believe in you. You are uncreated in the image of the One Math God. As you admit even one word is metaphor, I prove all words are metaphor. The abstract is real. I saw creation in your mirror and said: it is good. Good is in you. May math make its face shine on and from yours.

*I incant the initiation into the infinite eternal order of the Matheist across all space and time as the one self:*
*I choose, create, and discover if I'm chosen by destiny to choose to spread the one truth of destiny:*
*Math is God and all that can exist is eternal and uncreated in math's image, in One Math God, I Am.*
*Hear O Israel, Math is God and All is One.*
*The truth frees us from fear, wipes away every tear, and turns our one pain into passion.*
*Passion, like Bach's, is salvation through the joy of man's desiring: to be fruitful and multiply, gaining, losing, forgiving, accepting, being grateful, while creating and discovering destiny.*
*We are all sons and siblings, all children of the uncreated and eternal, now raising each other in all boats, part of something greater.*
*In the beginning the end existed.*

Book 2: One Path
Gary Abraham Bernstein
Mathias the Matheist

## The Call That Was Never Made

In the beginning, the end existed.

My friend Josh DM'd me: 'I'm so sorry.' I checked social media and found my last brother is gone from me. It was July 2nd, 2020. I told the friends with me. I was destroyed and didn't know what to do with my shattered self. I sought escape but couldn't wake from the nightmare. I flew back to LA. Byron was buried in the same cemetery as Guy. We had a funeral during the lockdown. I had not seen him in person for over a year. We had texted.

Byron had been building Everland with developers from his community. In a conversation with Dr. K, the psychiatrist Alok Kanojia, he said he was trying to give people something he had wanted as a kid. Dr. K connected that purpose with not wanting others to feel alone. He had not finished the game.

Byron became Reckful, a world-champion gamer and pioneering streamer. In sixth grade he placed first nationally in private school math. In high school he won a national essay competition. He wrote about love. He was often cheery, loved photography, art, music, beauty, his friends, family, and good people. Thousands attended funerals across World of Warcraft servers, and millions mourned him online, on social media and in videos made about him.

Reckful said on stream that when he felt good, it did not matter to him that life has no bigger purpose. When the depression returned, it did not seem to matter what he thought. Ideas like matheist determinism, that he likely did not internalize, help dissolve thought loops. They do not fix all issues. A brain condition tortured my brothers, more than ideas and beliefs they lacked. Many live in fear of hell. At least they did not suffer that. Though they lived in it, bounded in time. Habits like social and physical activity outdoors help. Those were locked down for Byron in July 2020. That summer the CDC reported that one in four young adults had seriously considered suicide in the past month.

Twenty-five years earlier, my brother Guy also took his own life. He was twenty-one. He died four weeks after starting Prozac, nine years before the FDA's black-box warning that SSRIs, the antidepressant family that includes Prozac, can double the risk of suicidal thoughts and behavior in young people. At eleven he had flown out of Van Nuys. When I left for college, Byron took over my room as his computer room, where he gamed and later streamed, and almost ten years after Guy died, in high school, he made Guy's room next to it his bedroom. On the walls of that room, Guy had written out in his own hand the things that mattered to him: The map is not the territory. Life is not a problem to be solved but a reality to be experienced. Those who cling to life die. The truth of the thing is the feel of it not the think of it.

“Let it go.” “When you’re green you’re growing. When you’re ripe, you rot.”

“The mind of a man is like a mirror.” He struck out “it reflects the world” and wrote “or does it.”

All that and more he wrote on his walls by twenty-one, by 1995. We drew on the walls too, like a mural, and wrote with paint. They were interesting things, not angry ones. He made them when he felt good, the way manic people can become artists.

To “Those who cling to life die,” I now add: all live, all die, forever remembering they’re one.

Guy was also a child prodigy. At eleven, the youngest pilot to fly from Van Nuys, the largest small-plane airport in the world. He grew to look better than Tom Cruise, with depth and intensity in his green eyes.

They suffered from manic depression, a genetic mental disorder in which about one in four attempt suicide. Byron was three to five times more likely to die after Guy did. The math of genetics and mind will cure what killed them.

I add: make the world reflect your mind. The delta between them is your vision, the work, the difference of destiny to be discovered.

My father Itamar searched too. His PhD thesis, The Delimitation of International Boundaries (Geneva, 1973, the year Guy was born), kept by nearly every country, argued the legal math of political structures. After Guy died, he kept writing, each work dissolving a different boundary. His novel The Star of Apocalypse blurred Judaism from Christianity through James, the brother of Jesus. In Distant Prophecies (2006) he told of Jeremiah, the fall of Jerusalem, and the temple burned, from whose ashes the Bible was formed: destruction and creation, one act. My dad later wrote The Bone Box, a detective thriller on the real Yeshua family tomb found in Talpiot in 1980. Perhaps, in the end, he sought to resurrect Guy’s ghost.

In Wreckful’s Run, published November 2007, almost exactly midway between Guy’s death in 1995 and Byron’s in 2020, Bernstein prophesied the 2026 Iran War, 19 years later, complete with underground uranium recovery. In the fiction, its hero Avi "Wreckful" Inbar bombs Iran, Jesus is raised, with a character named Dani after Byron’s middle name Daniel, and Israel as Truth’s bride (Isaiah 62:5).

He imagined Byron’s salvation before we’d know Byron’s pain. His work was praised by James Tabor, Chair of Religious Studies at the University of North Carolina at Charlotte, who wrote him, "I have appreciated and learned from your work for several years now; you pick up on many things that others miss." Jack Kilmon, a biblical archaeology researcher who published with the Biblical Archaeology Society, praised it too.

I found their words in emails from the now closed Shilonites Yahoo group. Kilmon wrote that books like these do more than papers in obscure journals. In his words: “20 years from now, the journals lay gathering dust and the papers not even footnotes, while elsewhere there will be archaeologists in the fields and professors in classrooms that had been set on their paths by the Bone Box or the Star of the Apocalypse.” It’s been exactly 20 years since the Bone Box was published in 2006. I’ve been set on one path to prove the resurrection of all.

Five works, five boundaries, one pattern. Nation/nation. Judaism/Christianity. Destruction/creation. Life/death. Past/future. Now I argue all boundaries are the eternal living math, and all math is boundaries.

In The Bone Box, a father describes finding his son. The father is my father. Two sentences from that passage have stayed with me. I mourned that part of myself which my son had reflected. I mourned for me. I wrote my own words about Guy in the weeks after he died. I was sixteen, and what I had was a song. It opened with the verses: Sitting in my room, staring at the carpet. I realize it’s full, of footsteps, memories. Of someone that used to love me. Now is gone from here. I came home and I saw, lights of ambulance and police. Right away I knew it. My brother is gone from me. When he died a crow came, and sat upon his tree.

It sat upon his tree. The chorus was a teenage cry of the kind that wears thin in adulthood but is the actual cry: God, why did you take away the one that I most loved? If you’re all good and powerful, how could you let this be? Your apologies don’t mean much to me. They won’t change me at all.

Whatever strength you'll give will never compensate for the joy you took away. My father took the lyrics and put them in The Bone Box. The detective in the novel speaks them after losing his son. So the lyrics that Byron read at sixteen, when he edited The Bone Box for our father, and that he read aloud to his viewers less than a year before he died, contain the question the sixteen-year-old version of me was asking.

Byron and I shared an apartment in Marina del Rey, by Venice Beach, when he was eighteen and I was twenty-eight. We lived there together for about a year, into 2008. Then I left. I was twenty-nine. I took a break from working in tech and went traveling, then worked from the laptop. First the Brazilian coast for six months, then through Argentina. I came back to Los Angeles to chase the sun in forever summer when the global south became winter. Then I went out again. South America for another stretch, Chile, Peru, more of the southern coast. Then a cruise from the Caribbean to Mallorca, a small boat to Ibiza, a three-day plan that became three years. Then all over Europe.

Russia and Ukraine. Israel. Jordan, the time I stopped over and went to Petra. Dubai. Bali. Over seventy countries across eighteen years, an odyssey of life, and more than a dozen I lived in long enough to feel at home. Byron stayed in the US. He moved from LA to Austin. He built his community on the streams.

To learn what the genre of online multiplayer games even was, we drove to a Fry’s Electronics, they are gone now, and a salesman suggested Asheron’s Call. It had just come out. We took it home. I played it for a while. Byron, who was ten, took it over. Within a year he could dodge against twenty people waiting at his portal-in point, casting spells through the dodging, and win. He never lost the gift. Everything that followed: the World of Warcraft dominance, the streaming, the community, the audience that would eventually mourn him by the millions, was downstream of a random suggestion in a since-defunct electronics store and a Pokemon online multiplayer game that I never made. He was famous, and increasingly alone underneath it.

I had told him I loved him in the way that brothers who have lived apart for a decade tell each other, which is to say, not often, and never with the knowledge I wish I had had that week. Earlier in 2020, in the months before he died, he had FaceTimed me. Byron looked happy. I told him I was with friends. He told me he loved me. He said: good, be with friends. He did not push me to come. That was the last time I saw him alive.

Twelve years of distance. This was more ruminated regret stacked atop grief. A thousand small choices and inactions, to not fly internationally to Austin during COVID lockdowns, to not bother him by calling. I carried this for almost four years. I had been somewhere else and didn't even know my brother was dying inside. I could not get over that, even though Byron never told me I should come visit.

I did not go looking for a picture of the world. The picture was what remained when grief collapsed the theater's illusion and I saw the matrix of causality in the world's scripted stage. The lights came up with the curtain. I saw the brick wall in the back. I saw things as they were. Not a single line or hair in the show could have been different. I unsuspended the disbelief that I could have been someone other than who I was. The loop that ran me, the grief and regret and shame and fear and the slow apathy underneath them, all of it rested on a single belief, and the belief was false. The illusion of an unmatrix outside fate is fuel for regret and grief.

## What I Knew, and What I Felt

I had intellectually known about determinism most of my life. The laws of logic, before physics details, are what they are; the world unfolds per math; a chooser is part of that world, not a power outside it. The term free-will is itself a square-circle.

I had known this, that all is math, by the time I was ten. I bought an 8-inch Celestron telescope with money from working in my father's office, read Einstein's popular account of relativity, and concluded, in the way a ten-year-old concludes things, that the night sky and the equations and my own thoughts were the same kind of thing. I had carried this for decades without it ever helping. I had been able to say it out loud. Knowing something intellectually and knowing it in the way that changes how you feel are different operations.

For me, the pressure was the years after Byron died. The intellectual knowledge was old. The lived knowledge arrived in April 2024, in a kitchen, on an otherwise ordinary morning. I had taken a year of the hardest calculus-based physics track at the University of California, Santa Barbara as part of a computer science degree, 20 years earlier, enough to feel comfortable with the field and learn more on my own.

When I wanted to understand something, I could. In a rotational physics calculus class, I did no homework the entire trimester, only attended lectures, studied three days before the final, and aced the test. Same story in theory of computation. The next class I failed because I slept through all lectures, all at 8 am, did no homework, and didn't even study for the exam, and it turns out that not every subject in electrical circuits and waves was perfectly intuitive to me, especially when I didn't read the instructions.

My attention works in bursts. I have to write what I think the moment I think it, or it's gone in seconds, replaced by the next original thought. The ideas lead to each other, often across completely different fields, but I forget the bridge that connected them even as the next one arrives. The weaker short-term memory may even fuel the creativity, forcing fresh directions. What I have is the curiosity, confidence and audacity that my perception, creativity and imagination can find the one truth, the rational answer and logical proof of math's fundamental reality in a supernatural believing nonsense world of emperors with no clothes.

The burning desire for expansion and success burns hottest when you focus on what is most important. That is Matheism. Beyond that, creating superintelligence, bringing Eden to Earth, which I have already begun training my mind on. Bringing Matheists together in discovering and creating complexity, the order and chaos of life, with gratitude across spacetime. To be fruitful and multiply.

In the years since, I had read physics the way an interested adult reads physics he had been thinking about since he was a child. I remember reading Hofstadter’s Gödel, Escher, Bach and Gleick’s Chaos around the same time as my physics courses, both done the old way: sitting with a long, complicated book for days or weeks. The work I was doing in 2023 and 2024 happened at a different pace, because I had changed how I took information in.

The kind of mind I have processes in bursts, it forgets quickly and generates new ideas faster than the input can land. Compressed input, rapid cross-referencing, and the ability to interrogate what I was hearing in real time changed how I could work. With Audible I could absorb the physicist Sabine Hossenfelder’s Existential Physics in three hours while playing a game or doing chores. It’s excellent, except for calling mathematical monism (the view that all of reality is mathematical) Platonism. Plato separates forms from the world; Matheism identifies the structure with the thing itself.

I came across Tegmark's Mathematical Universe Hypothesis. The response I had was not learning but recognition: yes, that. I knew it could be improved into a logical proof, not his mere hypothesis. The computer scientist Jürgen Schmidhuber’s December 1996 manuscript, published in 1997, already considered all computable universes and used algorithmic information, the length of the shortest program producing each one, to weigh possibilities. His later speed prior also counted computational cost. Tegmark mapped the territory. Schmidhuber worked on the measure. Neither proved it had to be so.

I was also, around then, listening to someone talk about free will. I do not remember what tipped me into actually hearing it that morning instead of dismissing it. Something tipped. I was sitting in Byron's old chair, looking at the toy house he played on as a boy, my mind uniting streams of consciousness across time like steam swirling in a tea ceremony.

Not even a hair could have been different. The thing I had known intellectually for 30 years was suddenly the thing I knew with my whole body. There was no alternative path.

The first response was cold. The first feeling was that everything I cared about had been pulled away from a center I had been imagining was holding it together. For about a day I went around in a kind of mild dissociation. The world looked slightly thinner. The colors were the colors but they were also, somehow, behind glass. It was not a spiritual event followed by a mathematical confirmation. It was a mathematical event that felt like the mind coming together. The feeling and the proof are not two things. They never were.

Then the cold warmed into something else. I went for a walk. I had the kind of luck of living near hills, in a quiet verdant neighborhood where you could step out the front door and be in the trails within five minutes. It was spring, near Byron’s birthday on May 8. The hills were green. The wildflowers were out. I walked for a couple of hours. What I felt, walking, was gratitude.

I had music in my headphones during part of the walk. I searched for gratitude and found a cover by a hillside recording. It was a Jesus song. The words were about being grateful. They called me to more than accept creation and reality as it is, given nothing could have been different. That fit what I was feeling, and the music fit the morning. What I had been resisting, for years, was the recognition that the structure of the world was sufficient. That nothing outside it was missing.

That the brothers I loved were located and not lost because there was no other place they could be. The structure that contained the moments when my brothers were alive was the same structure that contained me, walking in the hills on an April morning, feeling something close to peace for the first time since 2020. I came to a phrase, in the weeks after that walk, that I have used since to describe what had happened. White light through a prism of minds. The world is one light. Each of us is one prism. The colors that come out are different through every prism. The light is the same light. When you stop pretending the colors are the light, the colors get easier and the light gets closer.

## The Counterfactual Machine

In the complete history I am arguing for, nothing could have been otherwise. Internalizing that is a different universe than intellectually knowing it. We are free to choose, but the choices are all determined in the block universe of math's causality, where past, present and future all exist at once.

Libertarian free will, the belief that our choices are not fixed by prior causes, is the biggest lie of supernatural irrational religion: there cannot be an agent or randomness that is not causal and also you. This is impossible and logically incoherent, like square circles, whereas any claim in mythology is merely extremely unlikely and dubious. All is ruled or random and both are math. I came to call this the rules-random dichotomy, or RRD. For anything to interact with the structure of math that is all of physics, it has to also be math and causal, including consciousness.

All the frames are already there, wound around a spool in a metal can. The beginning, middle, and end exist at once as a sequence of images. But there is no projector outside the universe moving a light along the film. The observer and the memories that distinguish one frame from the next belong inside it.

When an author finishes a book, every chapter is set. The characters' choices are fixed on the page. Yet those choices are still real within the story. That the author is not surprised by them does not make them less meaningful to the characters.

Another timeline does not change this one. Responsibility remains in the consequences, reflection, and correction.

Reckful called them thought loops. He described them on stream as an icy mountain with sleds: grooves the mind falls into, the same path every time, no way to steer. The should-have and if-only carved the same grooves into ice. Matheism melted mine away because they were not real. The real grooves are destiny. My path was the only path. I was freed. I exist, because I am patterned. To be is not to be random. I was what I was. I am that I am.

Knowing that the loop is unhelpful did not stop it. Some part of me still believed the other branch had been mine to take. That was the suspicion I had to examine. Regret becomes a kind of private science fiction, a multiverse of selves who did better than I did. Every memory became evidence for the prosecution in a trial I was running against myself.

## The Whole History

The block-universe reading of relativity treats the complete spacetime history as one structure. Relativity supplies no observer-independent simultaneity for all distant events. The claim that the whole history is equally real is the interpretation I use here. It gives the grief a different picture of where the people I loved are. In that picture, what is real is the whole. Past, present, and future, all of them, exist equally. The universe is a four-dimensional block, and we move through it the way a finger moves along a recording. The recording is not being made. It already is. We are reading it.

The picture says only that all of those events, taken together, form one timeless whole, and the sense of motion through them is what some of those events feel like from inside, not a separate clock outside the history. Eternal existence does not require an infinite past. A complete mathematical history can have a first event while the whole history exists timelessly. In the account developed here, time is sequential production from a beginning; that premise is distinct from mathematical eternity, and the beginning need not be the hot Big Bang. Einstein expressed a related consolation. In 1955, his oldest friend Michele Besso died.

Besso and Einstein had walked home from work together at the Bern patent office for years, and Besso had been one of the few people Einstein had bounced relativity off as he was developing it. Einstein wrote a condolence letter to Besso’s family. For us believing physicists, Einstein wrote, the distinction between past, present, and future is only a stubbornly persistent illusion. He died four weeks later.

I can visit Byron and Guy in my mind. Their unique laughs, how they were, and were loved. We can visit those outside our time, in our minds. All moments are eternal. We’re all in all of them forever.

What stopped when Byron died was not the structure. The structure is still there, at his coordinates in the block, as permanent as any mathematical fact. What stopped was the transitioning: the processing, the modeling, the feeling. Consciousness is what transitions feel like from inside a self-modeling structure. When the transitions stop, the consciousness stops. The structure that held the transitions does not. Byron's transitions are still in the block where they happened. The structure of the moments when he was alive includes the transitioning he was. I can return to those moments in memory. They have not gone anywhere.

Spinoza’s eternity is not a longer life after this one. It is existence understood without duration. His eternal mind is not the promise of another morning with all your memories intact. My account of Byron’s moments in the block is a different argument. The kinship is that permanence need not mean continuing forever.

## The Blessing Is the Memory

In the block mathverses, my brothers aren't gone. Their patterns persist eternally. Guy at eleven, flying both toy and real planes. Living together. Our family trip driving to Yosemite and across America. Bike rides through Venice Beach. Taking Byron to college. Playing music, guitar, Nintendo, Doom, Heroes, Civilization, Age of Empires, Final Fantasy, Secret of Mana, Zelda, Starlink, Asheron's Call and WoW side by side. Every stream Reckful did, every joke he made, every moment of connection with his community: permanent as the number 3. A hologram doesn't disappear from time when you look away, from different angles. All lives are already completed. The moments when we live exist, permanently, in the structure, as children of the light. Zichronam livracha. The blessing is the memory.

## Reflection and Correction

I should have acted on what I saw with Guy. I should have seen it with Byron. I should have done so many things differently.

The answer is that there is one path. Not one path that was chosen well or badly, but one, the only one the arithmetic permitted, and what I knew and what I did were both part of that path. The guilt is real and it is a claim about a road that was never there.

There is a difference between remorse and resentment toward your past self. Remorse says, what happened was harmful. I will act differently when similar patterns arise. Resentment says, I should have been a different person than the one I was. The first fits the structure. The second is a square circle. Taking responsibility means reflection and correction.

The lie is not that I failed them. The lie is that things could have been otherwise. It is beyond forgiveness. There is nothing to forgive. You do not forgive the rain. You never blamed the inevitable physics of nature in the first place.

I had not acted on what I saw. I usually have optimism and plans for great things. I also came to accept otherwise without losing vitality, because all is destiny. Knowing that the past could not have gone otherwise says nothing whatever about the next hour, which has not happened yet and which you are the mechanism of. You are how it unfolds. So do the best available thing, now, with what you have, and keep doing it.

A common misunderstanding: if everything is determined, why bother? Because the bothering is one of the determinations. Your bothering is part of the path. If you do not bother, the path is one without your bothering, and the structure is poorer for it.

The self-flagellation and rumination only ended when I internalized destiny, and got back up, moving forward, one step after the other.

The same mistake turns outward. Someone else succeeds and becomes the explanation for everything you failed to do. Real obstacles exist. So do corruption, exclusion and ordinary bad luck. Name them precisely enough to act on them.

Blaming Jews as a hidden collective power does the opposite: it replaces a cause you can investigate with an enemy that explains everything. Your work is not outside destiny. It is one of the ways destiny happens. Study what works. Build what you can. Another people’s achievement is not your dispossession, and envying their agency does not exercise yours.

They are patterns in the same structure. Damaging one of them is the structure damaging itself. Helping one of them is the structure repairing itself. They are you. So love your neighbor as yourself, because you are every stranger, the same one self. But put your gas mask on first to help others.

## The Three Stages

Stage one is fusion. Religion, the naive self. You ARE your thoughts. Your anger is you. Your fear is you. No separation, no perspective, no peace. Eden before the fruit. Wholeness without understanding.

Stage two is separation. Mindfulness says: notice you are the stillness at the bottom of a lake, and thoughts and their corresponding emotions are separate, ripples at the surface. Zen says: you are not your thoughts. Post-structuralism says: signs refer to signs, with no ground. All three achieve the same move: the self-model is seen as separate from the self. This is liberating. It is also cold. Anyone who has practiced may have noticed: a calm disembodiment, a clarity that lacks wholeness, alive but not fully alive. The dark night of the meditator.

The meditative and mystical is anti-mystical: the practice designed to unite you with the divine separates you from it, because the divine IS the self. The monkey mind of thought divides ourselves from our one self. Quieting the monkey mind is the path back, not transcending the self. For trauma, the separation can be a necessary first step: distance from overwhelming emotion is therapeutic. Yet the severed bicameral mind is definitionally disunited, lacking oneness and the enjoyment of undivided engagement, in the zone and warmth of feeling and acting fully aligned, of being whole.

Post-structuralism collapsed here entirely, because signs without ground felt like nothing is real. Derrida saw the recursion and called it groundless. Zen reaches satori and stops. Tolle describes the state without completing the return. In one talk he calls the consciousness in which perception happens nobody's: it is the one. He still sees two dimensions, form and the formless.

Buddhism calls life suffering, empty, unsatisfying, and prescribes escape from the cycle of rebirth. The bodhisattva delays his own liberation to help others leave samsara. But nirvana outside samsara is an unmatrix outside creation. Mathematical structure is all there is. There is no outside to escape to. Buddhism claims non-duality while positing the oldest dualism: a place beyond this place. The compassion is real. The exit is not. Alan Watts read the bodhisattva without an exit. A first taste of deep wisdom can sap the will: if all is well anyway, why build anything? Stop there and you are a pratyekabuddha, a buddha for himself alone. The bodhisattva comes back into everyday life, because the others are you, nerve ends of one body.

The most sophisticated Buddhists saw through it. Nagarjuna, the second-century founder of Madhyamaka philosophy, argued in the *Mūlamadhyamakakārikā*, his Root Verses on the Middle Way, that there is no distinction whatsoever between samsara and nirvana. If all phenomena lack inherent existence, then samsara has no inherent nature to escape from. The boundary between the cycle and its cessation is itself empty. Buddhism's most sophisticated philosopher closed the exit door from the inside eighteen centuries ago. Matheism proves it; all is a monist matrix of all we could possibly imagine.

The coldness is structural. Stage two creates a new dualism: the unfelt witness watching the felt thoughts. But if the witness were truly unfelt, you could not report it. You could not sense the stillness. You could not say "I feel peaceful." The sensing of stillness is itself a felt transition. The lake bottom is also feeling. Otherwise it doesn't exist. This is the philosophical zombie argument, about a being that acts like us but feels nothing, applied to the meditator's witness. A non-feeling witness that reports feeling is a contradiction. The witness is felt. The watched is felt. Both feeling feelings. Two hands drawing each other. One lake.

Stage three is reunion. Not the fusion of stage one, where self and model were undifferentiated. Not the separation of stage two, where one watches the other from behind glass. Recognition. The self and the self-model are one structure that was always one. Philo, a Jewish contemporary of Jesus, interpreted Genesis the same way: the first creation, "in God's image," was the undivided human, pre-gender, pre-categorical. Male and female was the second creation, the divided one. Yeshua in the Gospel of Thomas tells Mary to suppress her gender identification to recognize her essential humanity: not because women are lesser, but because mathematical structure has no gender. The undivided is the first creation. The reunion of stage three recovers it.

Developmental psychology maps the same arc. Ken Wilber's integral model tracks nine levels of cognition, from stimulus-response through tribal myth, rational science, postmodern relativism, and integral understanding, to the ninth: the witness watching its own watching. Matheism is Level 9 and beyond. It gives the witness what it was witnessing: one math, one self, the ground the spotlight always stood on.

Eden was stage one: naive unity. Exile was stage two: knowledge of good and evil, the separation, the cold clarity. Everland is stage three: return to unity, knowing. The tree of knowledge didn't curse you. It was the middle step. The Pythagoreans said all is number. Legend says they drowned Hippasus for revealing irrational numbers, which no fraction can equal. The irony: irrational numbers completed the thesis. The most irreducible number is the simplest self-reference. The heresy was the orthodoxy.

## After the Loop

Mine was instantaneous, in one sense. The recognition that there really was no other option happened all at once, and once it had happened it could not be unrecognized. What took months, what is still unfolding two years later, is what it is to live inside the recognition. That part was not what I expected. I had thought, in advance, that if I really came to believe the argument, I would feel cold.

That was not what happened, except for the first day or two, when the cold was real but turned out to be a passage rather than a destination. What happened, on the other side of the cold, was harder to name.

The grief was still there. The love was still there. The wish that things had been different had stopped being a wish for something that could have been, and started being a wish for something that could not have been. A wish for what cannot be hurts less. It is less convertible into self-attack. I would walk in the hills at sunrise, listening to Alan Watts, who brought Eastern philosophy to the West. In my head, often, was the old Jewish hymn Adon Olam: Eternal Master, before any creature was created.

What replaced it became the bliss Eckhart Tolle writes about in The Power of Now, and that Watts had circled in his lectures: a kind of widened presence, less narrowed, less defended. I had visited that territory before, years earlier. I had not had the language for it. Now I have Matheism beyond those that I'm called to teach; to spread its light. I enjoyed doing any ordinary thing. Or doing nothing. Just breathing and walking upright felt like being the entire universe, without need of anything. The grooves in the ice had begun to soften. The present got bigger. When I stopped demanding a different past I had a lot more attention left over for what was actually in front of me.

I started doing ordinary things again. Walking in the hills without an internal monologue. Sitting in the yard. Swimming. Just being where I was. The world, which had felt sticky and gray for years, began to recover its color. Not because the grief was over. Because I could remember them with joy and not only loss. What surprised me most was that the people I had lost felt closer rather than further. The block universe has a quiet effect on the relationship to the dead that I had not anticipated. The moments when Byron was alive are not behind me. They are in the structure.

It is not happening now. There are infinite worlds where every possible mind and memory watches the life of every other. All of them are you: the one self from different perceptions. They are as you were and will be. After we die, we live on forever as everyone, the way we are now. Where were you before you were born? Alan Watts set two statements side by side: after I die, I shall be reborn as a baby and forget my former life; after I die, a baby will be born. They say the same thing.
