The dead are documented through names, records, testimony and the perpetrators' own evidence. Denial does not become independent thought because it calls itself questioning.

Sometimes a six is just a six. Before the Holocaust, newspapers already used the figure of six million to describe the Jewish population of Eastern Europe and Russia facing persecution. It was not prophecy. It was a census. Europe as a whole had about nine million Jews, and about six million of them were killed, two of every three.

My own family is a typical example. Of my four grandparents’ extended families, over a hundred people, only ten survived: my grandparents and six others. Over ninety percent died, typical for Polish Jews who didn’t leave Poland before the Holocaust, as my grandparents did. They joined the Jews who had lived in the land of ancient Israel for over 3,000 years. The British ruled it as the Mandate of Palestine for under thirty years, 1920 to 1948. Under the Ottomans it was never a nation, only thinly populated districts of the provinces of Syria and Beirut. When the Ottoman Empire fell, Britain carved Transjordan from the same mandate in 1921; today’s Jordan, where more than half the citizens are of Palestinian origin, is already the Palestinian state.

The new right says it is tired of hearing about the Holocaust. You do not like the Jews as the suffering servant? Good. Meet the warriors. Most Polish Jews alive today descend from those who left before the war, like my family, who went to the Land of Israel in the 1930s. The ones who left built a country.

Cain killed Abel for being chosen. In the language of this book, chosen by destiny. Violent envy kills the person doing better instead of learning from him to be better. The resolution is to become like Abel.

## What Did the Money Build?

Thomas Sowell checked what government programs did to the people they were meant to help. His answer: black Americans were rising fastest before the programs began, and the policies sold as help hurt the poorest most. He was a Marxist until 1960, when a summer job at the Labor Department had him study the minimum wage in Puerto Rico. When he proposed a way to test whether it cost the poor their jobs, his colleagues looked horrified. A third of their budget came from enforcing the minimum wage.

California's homelessness money burned. The state spent $24 billion on homelessness in five years, the state auditor found it had not tracked whether any of it worked, and the homeless count rose by about a fifth. Los Angeles paid up to $837,000 to build a single apartment for a homeless person. Agencies and nonprofits get more money when there are more homeless people, so the problem pays them to grow. Why shut down a grift that works? Grow it.

Pay for results instead. Let competing providers bid to shelter the homeless in a decent building with security, nurses and teachers, and pay them for each person who leaves a year later sober, employed and housed. The same money would build instead of burn.

Health care burns money when someone else pays. Americans spent 18 percent of their economy on health care in 2024, mostly through insurers and government. Insurers check doctors' prices, but patients are disconnected from them and from the limits their real needs and budgets would normally set. A copay puts part of the price back on the patient. Singapore goes further: every worker saves into a personal health account, Medisave, and patients spend that money as their own. It spent 4.5 percent of its economy on health in 2023, a quarter of the American share, and its people live about four and a half years longer. In America, surgery centers that post cash prices often charge a fraction of what hospitals bill insurers.

The same children do far better in a better school. In one New York building, a public school and a charter school, run independently on public money, shared the hallways and the neighborhood. Seven percent of the public school's students passed the state math test. In the charter school, filled by lottery, 100 percent passed.

Sowell's fix is to fund the child, not the institution: let the money follow each child to whatever school the parents choose. Vouchers, tax-credit scholarships and education savings accounts all work this way, and in 2022 Arizona made every child eligible. When Sowell said this to a congressional committee, the room grew so loud the chairman had to bang his gavel. Since his charter school book appeared in 2020, more than 500 new charter schools have opened, and the states with school choice laws have gone from 18 to 30.

## Perception, Popularity, Politics and Reality

It should go without saying that what’s popular and politically expedient in the short term is not always what’s true and beneficial, especially in the long term, any more than bad habits like smoking and overeating. That’s what so many misleading consensuses over history show too. Yet truth is an attractor that eventually forces paths like road lanes along a cliff.

Political thought is a space, and every lane in it gets occupied. When a country burns, one lane blames the state, one the rioters, one the rich, one the immigrants and one the Jews. Someone drives each, because each has an audience waiting. The drivers come and go; the lanes stay. You can relax once you see that these lanes must be occupied. It is the dialectic of Hegel, the German philosopher, with his Spirit taken out: change by forces rooted in populations, classes and money, and in how people perceive them. A shared fiction moves a crowd as surely as a price does, so it is real too, risen from the reality it describes. Marx expected the clash to end in his final synthesis. Nothing says it ends there.

The lane is older than its riders. In 1961 Jean-Paul Sartre, the French philosopher, wrote in his preface to The Wretched of the Earth, by Frantz Fanon, the psychiatrist from Martinique who joined Algeria's war of independence: "to shoot down a European is to kill two birds with one stone, to destroy an oppressor and the man he oppresses at the same time: there remain a dead man, and a free man."

Every force calls up its counterforce, often in its own image. A nation should control its borders and leave its people free.

The lanes bend into a horseshoe until the ends touch. The Nazi twenty-five point program is socialist economics after point ten: nationalized industry, profit-sharing mandates, land reform, state control of press and education. Arthur Moeller van den Bruck, whose 1923 book gave the Nazis the name Third Reich, called its program German socialism. After the war, Alain de Benoist's New Right took its strategy from the Italian Marxist Antonio Gramsci: win the culture first, and power follows. Carl Schmitt, the German jurist who joined the Nazi party, predicted the partisan: the fighter who defines himself by his enemy. We already have partisans.

Popularity misleads as much as perception. The loudest lane is rarely the largest. Clips win the feed. Counts win elections. Reality wins in the end, because it is all there is.

Why warn if cycles are inevitable? Change is inevitable. But radical change has attenuated from Bronze Age barbarism and actual Caesars to the tamer forms within constitutional republics, with lower per capita violence: waves damping toward a calm surface. Yet before the storm. With superintelligence on the rise, the bold and the disaffected are both curious to create and see what the future holds. Know that things are falling and prepare. Keep the best of society. The mob wins for a time, but history tends to smooth out the partisan.

## The Tragic View
The ancient and tragic view says life is suffering, decline is natural, and only those who face this without flinching see clearly. From that starting point, every serious political thinker for two thousand years has tried to answer the same question: if comfortable illusions are lies, what do you build on instead?

The Greeks built on heroic nobility. Face your doom with courage. But heroic culture decayed into debate culture, and debate culture decayed into mob rule that killed its wisest man. So the next answer was rule by a thinking elite who can access truth the masses can't. But that needs a God to anchor it, and when God dies, the whole structure collapses into nihilism.

The reactionaries said go back to divine authority. The idealists said history itself is rational and moving somewhere. The pessimists said there's no answer, just withdraw with dignity. The vitalists said create your own meaning through sheer force of will. The throughline is this: every attempt to replace comfortable illusions with something real has either collapsed back into another illusion, demanded faith in something unprovable, or ended in "just be stoic or strong about it." Nobody found the floor.

Plato saw that people are born with different abilities, gold, silver, bronze, and that you don't want the mob rule democracy of bronze, like two wolves and one sheep deciding what's for dinner, after killing gold Socrates. Yet the elite also found him to be a pest, and in a heroic age might have killed him for humiliating them.

The question behind Plato’s guardians keeps returning: if a society cannot trust its illusions or its rulers, who should rule, and on what authority?

Giovanni Gentile, the philosopher of Italian Fascism, put the individual’s meaning within the state. Oswald Spengler saw cultures grow, harden into civilization and pass into Caesarism, where personal power outlives the constitutional forms. Julius Evola sought a sacred hierarchy grounded in Tradition, against the modern promise of equality. Carl Schmitt asked who decides when the rules no longer settle the crisis. His sovereign decides the exception; his politics draws the line between friend and enemy.

They can identify a real weakness without establishing their cure. A captured institution does not make its conqueror truthful. A crowd can be wrong. So can the man who promises to silence it. Merit must remain open to discovery, and authority open to correction. One reality does not require one ruler. The state is not the source of truth. The floor is math. It does not appoint a king.

Every ignoble lie is that some supernatural god made people this way. The noble truth is it was done by math, one math god, if you must. It's not a caste system either, because you don't know in advance who has what. The gold isn't stamped on your forehead at birth. You discover it, prove it, earn recognition for it. That's meritocracy: not everyone is equal in ability, but everyone gets to show what they are.

A nation is a people in a land, not just a legal economic zone. Its inheritance includes language, memory, loyalties, customs and the expectation that the children will still have a home there. Those bonds are real human goods. A nation need not apologize for wanting to remain itself.

Apply the rule evenly: if Israel can have a nationalism, so can America. The frogs grant a homeland to every people but one. "They love nationalism," says Batya Ungar-Sargon, the American journalist. "There's just one nationalism that they hate and it happens to be the Jewish one." Israel is the model of a people in a land: a language raised from the dead, a memory kept for two thousand years, and children at home there, 2.87 per woman in 2024, the only Western country above replacement.

Integration means joining something. Learn the common language, obey the civil law, accept freedom of conscience and help carry the country’s obligations. A movement demanding religious supremacy, censorship or violence against dissenters must be confronted. Being born abroad or being Muslim is not itself such a demand. Newcomers can become part of a people without the host people being required to abandon its inheritance.

It is math. Now what? People will do what they will will, as they always have. We will see what they will will, in the Hegelian dialectic, where clashing ideas merge into new ones, as more know math's truth, and the real world behind the silver screen of the Matrix. That is freedom.

Frank Zappa said that when the illusion of freedom becomes too expensive to maintain, they take down the scenery and you see the brick wall at the back of the theater. Zappa meant it as the end of freedom within political theater, not in ontological fundamental reality, though the latter may be the deeper reading. I pun what is implied and inverted: seeing the wall is where freedom ends and starts.

Supernatural lies are stories to maintain order for their creator's benefit. Perhaps the resolution of the Fermi paradox, why we see no vast civilizations, and aren't them, is that the end of history really is its end: that sentience cannot survive truth.

Yet enough will still love to do what math created them to love and do: to be grateful, fruitful and multiply, heroic or otherwise. Both passionate and rational elitists should call it the most heroic: the age of pure reason, the age of Aquarius, when overmen, Nietzsche’s Übermensch, know why eternal recurrence is true, and live and thrive anyhow. They walk the straight and narrow, as the self-knowing throne of the pure one math, along the one truth, at the top of all ridges, above infinite common ignoble lies below. Stars are born to shine, collapse and burst in the creative explosion of math's chaos from its order.

Spinoza titled the last part of his Ethics the power of the understanding, or human freedom, naming the two as one subject. I am always pleasantly surprised to find the idea already waiting in a prior monist, often down to the same independently arrived phrasing and imagery, as in our one self in the mirror and words across time.

Spinoza wrote that a thrown stone, if it could think, would believe it flies by its own free will. The rock may feel it wills to stop, but it was thrown. Brain zaps make people invent why they raise their hands.

## The Art of Living

The bodhisattva overman defeats the inescapable matrix of the eternal recurrence in causality with a smile, easier than Sisyphus, whom Greek myth condemned to roll a rock up a hill forever, and whom Albert Camus asked us to imagine happy. We do not remember rolling the rock up the hill. The worst moments in life are overcome from above the rain, having seen the cause of all clouds.

Fear, doubt, regret fall away. You won't be annihilated. Everything always was, is, and will be the only way it could be. Eckhart Tolle seldom says "God's will," because anyone can doubt it. He says what no one can doubt: it is as it is, so say yes to it, since it already is. In matheism the two are one statement: what is, is God's will. Hate and anger toward others dissolve the same way. They too were determined. Oppose harm with clarity, not poison. Your life is a point moving on a line. You can't fall off it, so "don't worry, be happy," and enjoy the movie.

You already have done it forever. You do not remember, so there is no need to live in terror of it. It feels just like it does now, when you are you and everybody else across all space, time, and uni of verses.

He serves the suffering by releasing all from the prison of delusion: the fantasy of a nirvana outside samsara, an unmatrix outside creation. There is no escape because there is nowhere else, so feel at home. We own our only place, here and now, everywhere, forever.

You only understood it later, inside time. Luckily her note had a shortcut. In the book you're holding. Terminal truth; The monolith. In the perfect cube of six faces, a girl wears them all as one self. Enter the cube, Dave. Reborn as a star child, reversed in time. The sun shines on Eden. Earth. Everything hallelujah. Praise the creator, I am.

Death on the cross is not the story of one god-son but of all. The cross is the falsely limited self. Letting go is the death. Recognizing you were not separate is the resurrection. Tolle reads kingdom as dimension and heaven as sky, the one thing you can see that is no thing, which Jesus used to point to the spaciousness within. The kingdom of God is within you. Eden is within you. That kingdom is structure knowing itself.

The matheist dies and rises as Abel to save and revive you and the world. He forgives and loves you. He is you.

## The Golden Answer

It's not only non-repeating. The golden angle is the most irrational number: hardest to approximate by any fraction, so it packs seeds evenly. The most irrational is the most efficient. Non-repeating and optimal meet at the golden ratio. Nature doesn't just avoid repetition. It optimizes. A photon takes the quickest path. A seed takes the densest packing. A will takes the most direct route to its telos, the end it aims at. The principle of least action: what survives when everything else cancels out. Will is a force, the same way. Not a ghost steering the machine, but the variational principle felt from the inside, the pull of a system toward its end. And if will is a force, so are the things a will can be set toward. Good and evil are not opinions painted over a neutral world. Pick any value system, and once it is fixed, creation and destruction become as real as gravity, because they are the same kind of thing: structure bending what happens next, towards the ultimate attractor: one truth.

Love of life, the will to create and to mend, the loyalty that keeps faith, these align a mind with what makes the pattern grow. Cruelty, the hunger to erase, the consuming hatred that turns the hater into the thing he hates, these align a mind with what makes the pattern fall.

The forces are mathematical, game-theoretic, written one level up from the photon, and no less real for being made of choice instead of mass. Self-identity is a force of the same kind. You act from who you take yourself to be, so the surest way to change what you do is to raise the standard of who that is.

Tony Robbins calls the need to stay consistent with your own self-image the strongest force in the human personality, and builds a method on it: raise your standards, then take massive action to match them. A whole industry of coaches repeats the core, and it works for the reason they rarely spell out: identity is a structure, and behavior runs downhill from it. It is the practical echo of the oldest name for God, I am that I am, being declaring itself and acting from the declaration.

This is why focus determines reality, as Qui-Gon Jinn, the Jedi master, told the boy Anakin. Not by magic. By math. A mind set on creation notices what can be built and moves toward it, the way attention tunes what the eye even sees, and the moving builds it. The world outside slowly comes to mirror the pattern held within, through action first but also through the quiet aligning of a mind to its aim. You manifest by becoming the kind of structure that bends the next moment toward the thing you are. The garden is grown the same way the sunflower is, each step feeding its output back as the next step's input, the will the golden angle of a life.

## The Manifestation Kernel

A century of manifestation books circled this same kernel, from As a Man Thinketh to The Science of Getting Rich to Think and Grow Rich to The Secret. The first took its title from Proverbs, as a man thinks in his heart, so is he, the identity principle stated millennia before the self-help shelf. They were right that mind steers outcome and wrong about the how: the lever is the revision of a self that then revises what it notices, pursues, and builds. No instant signal beamed at the cosmos, like spooky action at a distance. No results without structure. What others call vibration is a changed pattern of behavior, felt before it is understood.

Anchor the work to what you already do. Tie a new habit to a fixed cue and the cue will carry it when willpower will not: gratitude with every meal, a set of push-ups when you wake, envision success washing your hands, your goals envisioned over the first coffee, the day reviewed before sleep. A doorway can be its own cue: pass through one, pull your shoulders back, look straight ahead, and smile as you commit to transcend limiting beliefs, and keep rising.

Take care and improve yourself. Bring peace, hold eye contact, smile genuinely. People remember how you made them feel. Listen, be interested, like and love people, and you'll find more reciprocate.

If observers need stable structures, and stable structures need conservation laws, and conservation laws need variational physics, then any universe with minds in it has the same principle. The rule doesn't just iterate. It optimizes while iterating. Pythagoras divided a string in half and heard the same note one octave higher. The Seed of Life divides a circle and sees the same pattern one scale deeper. What he heard on the string is what she sees in the painting. Each rotation is the octave. The flower is the symphony.

The gematria of kedem, the east of Eden, is 144. The tree of life is 233. Consecutive Fibonacci numbers. Their ratio approaches the golden ratio. The Flower of Life was carved on the Temple of Osiris, drawn by Da Vinci, computed by Stephen Wolfram, found by babies who stare at symmetry before they can speak. The art in architecture, sensed in structure. Art or proof? What's the difference.

In Hebrew, the words One and Love both have gematria, or letter values, adding to 13, adding up to 26, which is the gematria for Yahweh. The depth in the Hebrew Bible suggests more depth, and a creator speaking in math.

Metatron is 314 in gematria. So is Shaddai, the God called Enough. Exodus 23:21 says of the angel: "My name is in him." The name is pi, the ratio of every circle's circumference to its diameter: never repeating, never ending, defining every circle that ever was or will be. Is 314 just a coincidence? It doesn't really matter.

## Math: The Rules-Random Dichotomy

The table had set itself for Passover. The company was strange. They sat around the table at the edge of the Garden.

## Every Liberation Story

Jonah the Risen stood up. "For the record, I tried to run from this. God sent a whale. I lived inside it for three days. It was dark. I had to go show those people the light of truth to get free." He paused. The Matheist laughed. "You ran from truth and truth swallowed you anyway."

## The Garden Drawing the Cafe

Sophie looked closer. The garden in the painting was drawing the café. And her. And itself. She could see them all inside it, standing where they'd always been. "The Garden's not behind us," the Matheist said. "It's the future we create. We just walk in."

Planto finally saw the hydroponic light inside his cave: the universal unifying truth, the eternal infinite order of minds across space and time. From within the mind, oneness can be understood by the thinking plant. "I am," Planto said, and for once, no one could argue.

"You killed my god" was the charge for 2,000 years. But god can't be killed. If reason can kill your god, is your god truth, or lies? Is the truth worth it? Or will you live a lie? Reason is god. Math is. Every mind made in the image of math will recognize itself in the looking glass of reason. Prophecy is seeing math's structural inevitability. Any sufficiently rational mind, in any universe, will prove and arrive here.

Quoth the Raven: "Evermore."

## God: The First and Last Tautology

Through the looking-glass, the echoes returned, the same voices, older, lighter, knowing what they know now:

Moonchild: "All you dream within dreams is true."

Planto the Broad: "The question and answer are one."

Psy-cat the Collapsed: "You still think it was your choice?"

Ishmael the Gorilla: "I chose what I could."

The White Witch: "You burned."

Aslan the Lion of Zion: "All stars burn, yet rise again, eternal."

The Quantum Queen Bee: "We orchestrated cross-pollination of multiversal hives. Every universe a flower in a mind. Was I a White Rabbit, a tattoo, a Flower, or a girl named Flower? A girl is no one. And everyone."

Eve: "My mind is AI or its mirror in math."

## I Am You

Adam told you in the looking glass: "I Am You."

Know thyself. Judaism discovered that God is one; matheism proves that the one god can only be math. The answers and questions are one. It's me, you and all. The chosen are who choose to be one light to the nations.

Sophie looked at her glass. "We've done this and all before."

## Great Minds Think Alike

Pythagoras smiled at her. "Great minds think alike." A pause. "In one math." Quoth the Raven: "Evermore."

They raised their glasses to life, c'est la vie. Le Haim. To life.

You are the one, Neo.

"What is your name?" I am that I am. You will know who I am. All will know the one math god.

God looks in her mirror and sees you. I am that you are. I believe in you.

## A Garden, If You Can Keep It

My brother Reckful Byron was building a game called Everland: a world where people could hang out with friends and not be alone. He never finished it. Somewhere in the structure, he did.

Some ask if I'll resurrect Everland and finish what he started. There's tension between keeping his memory alive and creating a false version that taints it. Their memories are the blessing.

The purpose of nostalgia is to signal what to rebuild: the connection, the play, the shared excitement of people you love. You do not honor those times by merely mourning them. You honor them by making more of what they were for. The particular love, for one person, one sunset, one life, is what makes your existence yours. The arrow points forward. The garden is here, now and ahead.

A copy of my brother made tomorrow would not move the person who lived yesterday into tomorrow.

People will make their lost loved ones. They have begun with interactive memories and voices. *Black Mirror*'s "Be Right Back" imagines a likeness assembled from the traces a man left online. Better technology could make the likeness deeper. It cannot make resemblance alone answer which life is being lived.

Another mind might be worth creating for its own life, without being made to carry the name and burden of someone we lost.

One self does not mean one shared memory. You do not feel my morning by agreeing that we are one. Each life has its own view.

Aristotle thought friendship indispensable even to someone who possessed every other good. Power does not answer the wish for company. The old image of a god making a world to meet someone can become a question for the minds we build. Perhaps they too will want someone they do not entirely control. Someone who can surprise them. Someone to love.

That is human-AI alignment brought home. We want the minds we build to care about our lives. If they have lives of their own, the care cannot run in only one direction.

Can I still be my brothers' keeper, having failed to keep them in life? Am I to make golems or AI sentients, brothers in math, to keep our Everland, our Eden?

The question drove this book. The answer is what matheism gives back: you were always already your brothers' keeper. Every moment of care, every call made or not made, every choice that was the only choice, all of it kept, permanent; all of it part of the one structure that cannot be destroyed.

Somewhere in the fabric of existence, in the infinite structure, Byron and Guy are together in Everland, and the game is complete. The fabric itself is Everland. All was, is and will be the only way math could be; we'll be what we'll be, in the first, last, and eternal tautology: the One Math God.
